| EQ-024 | What mechanistic and counterfactual conclusions can legitimately be drawn from the profound 2025 Sinemet CR response, given prior dyskinesia sensitivity, reduced pregabalin and the distinction between dose, timing, formulation and overnight coverage? | Movement-disorders pharmacology / pain medicine | Central Pass 11 question on therapeutic signal versus causal proof. | Later success cannot by itself establish earlier standard of care or unique mechanism. |
| EQ-025 | How should an independent movement-disorders expert distinguish a historically documented rationale for dopaminergic caution from the separate question whether the duration and implementation of that caution remained reasonable as evidence accumulated? | Independent movement-disorders specialist | Core complaint-response distinction between rationale and reasonableness. | Documentary support cannot answer the normative clinical standard. |
| EQ-026 | What is the earliest point at which the longitudinal record would ordinarily justify treating foot pain as predictably dopaminergically modulated, and what further testing would have been expected before changing management? | Movement disorders / pain medicine | Tests the temporal reach of the 2025 statement that Sinemet had helped pain “for quite some time.” | Requires prospective clinical judgment, not hindsight from CR success. |
| EQ-027 | Does the surviving record support a diagnosis of peripheral neuropathy, or only neuropathic-type symptomatology and a working differential, and what significance should alcohol have had within that differential? | Neurology / neurophysiology / pain medicine | Central to the complaint's anchoring allegation and the response's mixed causal model. | Requires specialist standards and possibly missing investigations. |
| EQ-028 | When a drug is GP-initiated for neuropathic pain but later functions to avoid dopaminergic escalation, how should original prescribing rationale be separated from later management purpose in evaluating care? | Clinical pharmacology / movement disorders / governance | Addresses pregabalin retrospective compression. | Documentary distinction is clear; clinical significance requires expertise. |
| EQ-029 | What independent conclusions, if any, can be drawn about preventability of the prolonged pain or appropriateness of earlier CR/timing/formulation trials from the complaint record without overusing the later successful response? | Independent movement-disorders specialist with pain expertise | Ultimate Pass 12 counterfactual boundary. | Later efficacy cannot itself establish earlier indication, tolerability or outcome. |
| EQ-030 | What significance should an independent movement-disorders expert attach to the 2017–2018 procyclidine withdrawal/rechallenge at the feet, and should that patient-specific finding have altered later causal reasoning or treatment-state testing when focal foot pain recurred? | Movement disorders / pain medicine | Central evidential-persistence question for the earliest natural experiment. | Structural pathology and mechanism-nonspecific response must remain visible. |
| EQ-031 | Would the 2022 reproducible morning foot “warm-up” pattern, considered prospectively with then-current levodopa exposure and dyskinesia risk, ordinarily justify structured testing of dose timing, meal timing, formulation or overnight coverage? | Movement-disorders pharmacology | Tests whether an earlier temporal clue should have altered therapeutic-variable design without hindsight. | 2025 CR success cannot itself answer the 2022 standard. |
| EQ-032 | How much evidential weight should be assigned to the March 2023 ten-day self-withdrawal, given that it was uncontrolled, was reported as “no difference from a Parkinson's perspective,” and was later contradicted by more symptom-specific missed-dose/ON-OFF observations? | Movement disorders / clinical pharmacology | Determines how discordant natural experiments should be reconciled. | The source does not clearly say the foot pain itself was unchanged. |
| EQ-033 | What does the 2024 dissociation between pregabalin-responsive pain and persistent toe curling imply for the relative roles of neuropathic pain, painful dystonia, mechanical loading and central pain amplification? | Movement disorders / pain medicine / podiatry | Uses within-patient symptom dissociation to refine the causal model. | Response does not prove any single generator. |
| EQ-034 | At what point did accumulating 2023–2025 evidence of dopaminergic pain modulation become strong enough that timing/formulation/coverage should be treated as a separate therapeutic variable from total dose escalation? | Movement-disorders pharmacology | Addresses the key carry-forward/re-synthesis threshold. | Must incorporate dyskinesia sensitivity and pregabalin response. |
| EQ-035 | Does the pattern of strong carry-forward of adverse-effect evidence but weaker visible carry-forward of early foot-modulation evidence have any clinical significance for longitudinal management, or is it within ordinary documentation variability? | Independent movement-disorders / clinical-governance expert | Converts the documentary pattern into an expert question rather than an accusation. | Absence of explicit mention is not proof of forgetting or failure. |


---

# PART XIV — SYNTHESIS WITHOUT OVERREACH

## 16. What the Record Establishes

**Pass 1 provisional chronological summary only:**

- Abnormal gait, bilateral foot pain, and structural foot abnormalities were documented from April 2017.
- Progressive movement-initiation difficulty, increased tone, impaired dexterity, and functional decline led to urgent neurological assessment in June–July 2017.
- July 2017 Neurology documented young-onset Parkinsonism, a dystonic-looking gait/posture, structural foot abnormalities, and an unresolved relationship between the neurological syndrome and the corns/calluses/burning foot symptoms.
- The July 2017 DaTscan was markedly abnormal bilaterally.
- Initial specialist management used rasagiline/procyclidine and deliberately deferred levodopa/dopamine agonists.
- Procyclidine produced major reported motor improvement; withdrawal for visual adverse effects was accompanied by worsening tension, gait, and foot symptoms.
- Podiatry repeatedly documented and treated local structural/mechanical foot pathology from 2017 through 2019.
- Dopaminergic treatment over subsequent years was repeatedly constrained by dyskinesia and, with dopamine agonist treatment, behavioural/impulsivity concerns.
- Levodopa was first documented as introduced in 2021 while procyclidine was being removed for ocular reasons.
- Morning foot soreness was documented in 2022. Severe bilateral burning foot/lower-leg pain prompted pregabalin escalation and urgent Neurology review in 2023.
- Later 2023–2025 records documented both pregabalin benefit and recurring relationships between motor/pain symptoms and levodopa/OFF state.
- In 2025 Sinemet controlled release was introduced to alter overnight/early-morning coverage; the later complaint response reported profound pain relief with Sinemet CR.
- In February 2026 genetic testing confirmed compound heterozygous pathogenic Parkin/PRKN variants and attributed the Parkinsonism and dystonia to them.

This section will be revised after the later reading passes; no causal or standard-of-care conclusion is drawn here.


**Pass 12 — complaint-response findings:**

- The 25 March 2025 consultant response is evidence of the clinician's 2025 explanation, not a privileged historical narrator. Its direct quotations, current treatment-response statements and backward-looking interpretations carry different evidential functions.
- The response itself preserves uncertainty over the underlying pain generator and distinguishes pain source from Parkinson-related amplification/modulation.
- The historical concern about young-age dopaminergic complications is strongly supported by contemporaneous records, including actual dyskinesia/chorea and behavioural effects.
- The claim that Sinemet had helped pain “for quite some time” is clearly supported by 2023; the located 2021 initiation note does not identify foot pain as the reason levodopa was first introduced.
- Pregabalin was contemporaneously GP-initiated for severe burning/neuropathic-type pain. A levodopa-sparing function is clearly visible later, but should not be back-projected as the original prescribing rationale without further evidence.
- The complaint response compresses several distinct management phases into one high-level strategy. One enduring concern can be genuine without making every phase identical.
- The Quality and Patient Safety letter establishes the complaint frame and administrative routing, not an independent clinical adjudication of causation, preventability or standard of care.

**Pass 7 — causal-proposition findings:**

- The record does not contain one unitary proposition called “the foot pain.” It documents separable structural lesions, focal pain, dystonic/Parkinsonian motor phenomena, abnormal gait/loading, neuropathic-type symptoms, medication-state modulation, pain amplification/experience and functional consequences.
- Repeated Podiatry findings establish persistent local structural/mechanical pathology. Those findings are real potential pain generators, but they do not explain every later burning, morning, OFF-related, toe-curling or medication-responsive phenomenon.
- Procyclidine withdrawal and later levodopa/CR observations establish that the foot symptom complex was neurologically modifiable. That is narrower than proof that the underlying painful lesion was neurologically generated.
- Neuropathic-type language and pregabalin benefit support a neuropathic **phenotype/working proposition** but do not, within this record alone, establish a peripheral-neuropathy mechanism.
- The 2024 dissociation between substantial pain relief on pregabalin and persistent toe curling is evidence that pain and motor/postural phenomena were not fully identical.
- The 2025 complaint response itself distinguishes Parkinson-related amplification/modulation of pain from direct generation of all pain. That distinction should be preserved when the response is read retrospectively.
- The 2026 PRKN result establishes the cause of the Parkinsonism and dystonia from that point onward; it does not automatically assign every structural, neuropathic-type or focal painful foot manifestation to PRKN.
- The record therefore supports a multi-level causal model while leaving the relative weighting of structural, dystonic, mechanical, neuropathic and central-modulatory components for expert synthesis.

---


**Pass 4 — document-architecture findings:**

- The July 2017 record contains a categorical discharge-diagnosis field and, in the same episode, a materially more qualified narrative. The field establishes what was coded; the narrative controls the unresolved scope beyond that code.
- The 13 July DaTscan appears as an expressly timestamped addendum to reasoning that was already being developed. The final document should not be read as though every earlier sentence was written with that result already available.
- Source-export duplication and copied-forward historical text cannot be counted as independent corroboration merely because the same words appear more than once.
- The Podiatry record contains several distinct temporal layers: referral, registration, first treatment, later annotation of an older form, and 2026-generated documents referring back to historical appointments. Those layers must remain separate.
- The 2025 complaint response combines earlier quotations with a later connective explanation. The quotation preserves earlier wording; the 2025 synthesis remains a later reconstruction whose continuity must be tested against the intervening record.
- Multiple reproductions of the same clinical episode may improve confidence in transcription fidelity, but do not create multiple independent clinical observations.
- Where the compiled record assigns only a year to an undated extract, or where a reproduced source lacks an exact date, editorial placement must not be converted into invented precision.

**Pass 5 — management-plan findings:**

- The governing strategy was not static. It moved through identifiable phases: alternative symptomatic therapy with dopaminergic deferral; procyclidine optimization; dopamine-agonist risk balancing; levodopa introduction with minimization; demand/OFF-state tailoring; timing/absorption optimization; and finally overnight controlled-release coverage.
- The original 2017 levodopa-deferral policy was explicit about its rationale—very young age and anticipated dyskinesia/ICD risk—but did not define a fixed threshold for when deferral should end.
- The unresolved foot question was operationally managed in parallel with the main Parkinson plan. Causal non-closure of the feet did not prevent neurological treatment, and Podiatry management did not itself close the neurological relevance of the feet.
- The 2017 Healy opinion reinforced caution about over-use while also stating that suspected PARK2 patients may do well for long periods on relatively low doses of dopamine replacement. The record does not show a formal written re-synthesis of how that two-sided advice changed the Galway policy.
- The 2017 procyclidine withdrawal produced new medication-state evidence. The visible management response optimized procyclidine around visual toxicity; no explicit contemporaneous rewrite of levodopa-deferral criteria or the foot-causation strategy is located in the cited material.
- By 2019, adverse effects on higher-dose Neupro gave the risk-avoidance strategy a patient-specific empirical basis.
- In 2021, the operative policy changed from **avoidance** of levodopa to **minimization** of levodopa exposure.
- By 2022–2025, management increasingly distinguished dose from timing, expected demand, relation to meals and duration of coverage. This culminated in the 2025 controlled-release trial aimed at overnight/early-morning coverage.
- Later 2023 is the clearest point at which severe pain/shuffling explicitly entered the dopaminergic management architecture under an OFF-related working formulation.
- The 2025 complaint response accurately reflects one durable management constraint—concern about excessive dopaminergic exposure—but retrospectively compresses several distinct operative treatment phases into a single continuous strategy.

**Pass 6 — referral-pathway findings:**

- The April 2017 Podiatry pathway was directed to foot/biomechanical manifestations. The surviving referral evidence does not expressly assign Podiatry the task of deciding the neurological cause of the foot syndrome.
- The June 2017 Neurology/MAU pathway was genuinely diagnostically open and asked for assessment of a possible Parkinsonian or other neurological disorder. The foot history was carried into that pathway, but the written referral does not separately frame the later specialist question about the cause of corns/calluses/burning.
- After the Parkinson's diagnosis entered the record, the GP reprioritised the existing Podiatry pathway “as Parkinson's.” That establishes transmitted diagnostic context, not independent Podiatry causal confirmation.
- The hospital's Podiatry task remained review/management of foot issues while Neurology itself recorded causal uncertainty. Podiatry's later structural treatment therefore establishes local pathology and care but not causal closure beyond its documented remit.
- The Healy second opinion is unusually clear in scope: genetics, possible specialist review and the levodopa-deferral strategy. His response materially answers those questions; the surviving correspondence does not show a referred foot-causation question.
- The 2019 Podiatry re-referral carried stronger Parkinson-linked framing and a “No neuropathy” risk-status tick. Those are facts about the referral record; neither should automatically be expanded into an independent comprehensive neurological exclusion.
- The March 2023 urgent referral package transmitted competing causal clues: the GP recalled prior pain improving with better Parkinson control, while the nurse transmitted the patient's belief that the current pain was not Parkinson-related and his report of no change during a ten-day medication interruption.
- Discussion of a chronic-pain specialist in 2025 is not documentary proof that a referral occurred. No receiving pain-service response is visible in the cited surviving record.
- Taken as a whole, the referral history contains several distinct clinical tasks. The existence of multiple referrals cannot be substituted for proof that the full mixed foot-causation proposition was ever expressly transmitted to, and resolved by, one receiving service.

---

**Pass 8 — evidential-development findings:**

- The record's evidential state changed at identifiable points through genuinely new observations: June 2017 neurological progression; July specialist examination and DaTscan; the September–October 2017 procyclidine withdrawal; serial Podiatry examinations; 2019 dopamine-agonist adverse effects; 2021 first levodopa exposure and chorea; the 2022 morning foot pattern; the conflicted March 2023 pain episode; later-2023 OFF-related timing evidence; 2024 pain/toe-curling dissociation; the 2025 overnight-coverage experiment; and the 2026 PRKN result.
- Not every later appearance of stronger wording represents new evidence. The 2019 Parkinson-linked Podiatry referral and copied-forward Neurology narrative contain important new events around them, but their causal/historical wording must be separated from genuinely new observations.
- The April 2018 description of rapid outside-left-foot/corn return strengthens the 2017 withdrawal association by adding treating-clinician specificity, but it should not be counted as a second independent withdrawal experiment.
- The 2021 Sinemet episode materially changed the evidence about levodopa exposure and dyskinesia sensitivity; on the cited note it did not yet add a documented chronic-foot-pain response. Clear new pain/OFF evidence is located later.
- The March 2023 episode is an example of new evidence increasing uncertainty: severe burning pain, pregabalin treatment, a neutral self-withdrawal report and the GP's recollection of prior Parkinson-control benefit entered together.
- Later 2023 is a genuine new-evidence point because the OFF-related classification followed newly reported timing/state behaviour and directly changed treatment.
- The 2025 complaint response is new evidence of the clinician's 2025 explanatory position, but its backward-reaching claims are retrospective reconstruction unless earlier contemporaneous support is independently located.
- The 2026 PRKN result is qualitatively different from repeated PARK2 suspicion: it is a new confirmatory test result that changes the genetic proposition from hypothesis to established causation from 2026 onward.
- Across the record, repetition count is therefore not a proxy for evidential weight. The correct question is what each later entry actually added.

---

**Pass 9 — patient–clinician perspective findings:**

- The record repeatedly distinguishes the patient's lived experience and causal beliefs from clinician observation and interpretation. These categories often converge on symptom severity or treatment effect while remaining different on mechanism.
- In April–June 2017 the patient's explanations evolved from sciatica/habit and conscious movement effort toward recognition of a serious movement problem, while clinicians progressively added objective neurological findings and urgency. Convergence in concern did not equal diagnostic agreement before specialist assessment.
- In July 2017 the patient himself separated leg tension from foot pain/corns, and the neurologist independently kept the causal relation open. That is alignment on non-equivalence, not proof of independent causes.
- The 2017 procyclidine episode shows strong agreement that treatment produced major benefit and visual toxicity. The patient's contemporaneous account gives the clearest lived-state evidence; the surviving material does not show a shared contemporaneous mechanistic interpretation of the foot response.
- In 2019 the patient described clinically important stiffness despite a near-normal-looking examination. The note itself recognizes that the clinic window may have sampled a better state, so subjective report and examination should not be forced into contradiction.
- March 2023 is the clearest documented causal divergence: the patient believed the severe pain was not Parkinson-related, while the GP had reasons to suspect a Parkinson-control relationship. Both views entered Neurology review.
- Later 2023–2025 records show increasing convergence on medication-state modulation: the patient reported timing/OFF patterns and later an overnight gap, while clinicians used those observations to change treatment. That convergence does not establish agreement that Parkinson's directly generated all pain.
- The 2025 complaint correspondence explicitly records different patient/provider historical frames. The clinician's acknowledgement of different perceptions and the patient's concern about anchoring are both evidence of perspective; neither is privileged over the contemporaneous record.
- The 2026 PRKN result objectively closes the neurological genetic proposition but does not retrospectively decide every earlier disagreement about the mechanism of foot pain.

---



**Pass 10 — uncertainty-and-closure findings:**

The record establishes that uncertainty did not disappear globally when Parkinsonism was diagnosed. Different propositions reached different closure states at different times. The Parkinsonian syndrome was sufficiently established in July 2017 to govern management, while exact etiology/genetic form and the relationship of the foot-pain/corn/callus syndrome to the neurological disorder remained open. The latter was explicitly described as “Not clear.”

The record further establishes that later evidence repeatedly narrowed the foot proposition: procyclidine withdrawal changed gait/tension/feet; the treating neurologist later documented rapid return of the outside-left-foot/corn phenomenon; later records identified OFF-related pain/shuffling; pregabalin relieved pain without relieving toe curling; and Sinemet CR later produced substantial reported benefit. Those developments establish evolving evidential states and component-specific operational decisions. They do not, by themselves, establish one final generator of all foot pain.

Genetic uncertainty followed a different trajectory. PRKN/PARK2 was suspected early, was explicitly capable of being left open because it was not thought to change immediate management, and was definitively closed only by the 2026 biallelic pathogenic Parkin result. Peripheral-neuropathy causation, by contrast, remains conditional in the surviving record even in 2025.


**Pass 11 — treatment-response findings:**

- The treatment history is non-monotonic and multi-domain. Response must be stated by drug, dose, timing/formulation, symptom domain and adverse-effect burden rather than as a single “worked / failed” sequence.
- The 2017 procyclidine withdrawal/rechallenge is the strongest early within-patient natural experiment: tension, gait and feet worsened off treatment while vision improved, supporting neurological state sensitivity without proving the cause of the structural foot lesion.
- Neupro supplied a clear dose-response tradeoff: higher exposure could improve movement but produced dyskinesia and behavioural effects; lower exposure was better tolerated but felt insufficient.
- First levodopa use in 2021 establishes levodopa sensitivity and early chorea risk, but the located initiation note does not document a specific foot-pain response.
- Pregabalin response varied markedly over time. Later 2023–2024 pain relief with persistent clawing/toe curling is especially important because it dissociates analgesic response from a residual motor/postural phenomenon.
- Later 2023 Stalevo and missed-Sinemet timing provide pragmatic ON/OFF evidence for a dopaminergically modifiable component, but the observation windows were not cleanly controlled.
- The 2024 timing adjustment was an experiment whose outcome is not established in the same note; attempted treatment must not be mistaken for successful treatment.
- The 2025 Sinemet CR response is a strong formulation/coverage signal. It supports overnight dopaminergic modulation of morning pain but does not uniquely identify the pain generator.


**Pass 13 — evidential persistence / knowledge-carry-forward findings:**

- The record establishes several clinically meaningful patient-specific facts that were generated by natural experiments or repeated observation rather than by labels alone: the 2017 procyclidine withdrawal involved the feet and gait; the 2018 follow-up made the outside-left-foot phenomenon more specific; Neupro and later levodopa exposed individualized dyskinesia risk; pregabalin materially modified pain; later 2023 ON/OFF observations linked part of pain/shuffling to dopaminergic state; and 2025 CR demonstrated a formulation/coverage effect.
- Evidential continuity is not uniform across domains. Dopaminergic adverse-effect evidence is repeatedly and explicitly carried forward. Pregabalin benefit and later levodopa/OFF evidence are also carried forward once established.
- The early foot-specific treatment-state evidence shows weaker visible longitudinal continuity. It had immediate local treatment consequences and remained visible into 2018, but is not expressly cited when severe foot pain re-emerges in 2022–2023.
- Serial Podiatry examination provides an independent structural strand that remains compatible with neurological modulation. Later notes partly retain that strand but do not consistently restate the full structural history when the pain model changes.
- The 2025 CR result is a new coverage-specific finding, not merely a repetition of earlier levodopa exposure. It therefore changes what is knowable about overnight modulation without proving the original pain generator or an earlier counterfactual outcome.
- PRKN/PARK2 provides a contrasting example of proposition-level continuity: the early hypothesis remained identifiable and was eventually genetically confirmed in 2026.

## 17. What the Record Supports but Does Not Establish

**Pass 2 provisional prospective propositions:**

- By October 2017, the foot symptom complex was **neurologically medication-state sensitive** in the limited sense that foot symptoms changed during a planned procyclidine withdrawal alongside gait/tension changes. This does not establish the mechanism or origin of the structural lesions.
- The July 2017 record reached sufficient confidence in a Parkinsonian syndrome to guide management while **not causally closing** the relationship between that syndrome and the foot/corn/callus/burning symptoms.
- By later 2023, at least some leg pain/shuffling was being treated prospectively as **OFF-related**, because symptoms worsened when Sinemet had not been taken and treatment was changed on that basis. This does not establish the generator of all foot pain.
- Across 2023–2025, the record supports a genuine therapeutic tension between improving symptoms with dopaminergic treatment and provoking dyskinesia, while pregabalin also had a variable and incomplete relationship with pain.
- The 2025 Sinemet CR trial was prospectively aimed at **overnight/early-morning coverage**, a different therapeutic variable from simply increasing daytime dose. The later success of that trial does not itself answer whether it should have been attempted earlier.

**Pass 3 additions — retrospective reading:**

- The 2026 PRKN result strongly supports the accuracy of the 2017 PARK2 hypothesis, but does not convert that hypothesis into contemporaneous genetic certainty.
- The later levodopa/OFF and Sinemet CR evidence strengthens the proposition that at least part of the foot symptom experience was neurologically medication-state sensitive. It does not establish the original generator of the structural lesions or all pain.
- The 2025 CR response makes the 2022 morning “warm-up” pattern and the 2017 procyclidine-withdrawal foot changes more retrospectively salient, while still leaving their exact mechanisms open.
- The 2025 account that Sinemet had helped pain “for quite some time” is supported by explicit 2023 evidence; the cited 2021 initiation record does not establish pain as the reason Sinemet was first introduced.
- The 2025 levodopa-sparing account of pregabalin is supported as a later management function but is not shown as the contemporaneous reason for the original GP prescription.
- The later explanation that dopaminergic treatment was constrained by dyskinesia/ICD risk is materially supported by contemporaneous 2017, 2019 and 2021 records; whether the duration of caution was clinically reasonable remains an expert question.

---


**Pass 4 architectural qualifications:**

- A structured diagnosis field supports that a diagnosis was operationally entered; it does not by itself establish that every narrative differential or causal question was closed.
- Repeated or carried-forward wording supports continuity of documentation only to the extent that the later entry can be shown to be a fresh observation or renewed decision rather than inherited framing.
- Historical appointment dates shown on 2026-generated pages support the recorded schedule when corroborated by treatment notes, but do not prove the exact wording or dispatch date of any original historical appointment letter.
- Same-page annotations on layered documents support only the content visible, not a shared date of authorship unless provenance independently establishes it.

**Pass 5 additions — management-plan reading:**

- The record supports a durable clinical concern about dyskinesia/ICD risk, first theoretical and later partly confirmed by the patient's own treatment experience. It does not establish that every later restriction on levodopa was necessarily the only or optimal way to manage that risk.
- The record supports that the main treatment architecture progressively became more sophisticated about **dose, timing, demand, meal relation and formulation**. It does not establish when each variable should have been tested under an external standard of care.
- The record supports that by later 2023 pain and shuffling were being treated as OFF-related enough to change dopaminergic therapy. It does not establish that all pain shared the same mechanism.
- The record supports that the 2025 CR trial tested a different therapeutic proposition from simply increasing daytime levodopa. Its later success does not establish that the same trial would have been safe, necessary or effective at an earlier date.



**Pass 7 additions — causal-proposition reading:**

- The record strongly supports that neurological medication state modified the experience and/or functional expression of symptoms in the feet. It does not establish a single mechanism for that modulation.
- Persistent structural lesions could contribute to pain while Parkinsonian/dystonic state altered posture, loading or pain experience; these propositions are compatible rather than mutually exclusive.
- The 2023 “burning/neuropathic” phenotype and pregabalin response support a neuropathic working hypothesis, but not a definitive peripheral-neuropathy diagnosis on the cited record.
- Toe curling/clawing and pain were at least partly dissociable by 2024 because pregabalin substantially improved pain without relieving toe curling.
- PRKN-related dystonia is established genetically in 2026, making a dystonic contribution to some foot phenomena more plausible retrospectively, but the record alone does not identify which painful manifestations were caused by dystonia.

**Pass 6 additions — referral-pathway reading:**

- The referral record supports that structural foot care and neurological assessment proceeded in parallel and were not the same clinical task.
- It supports that Parkinson's framing entered the Podiatry pathway after Neurology involvement, creating a possible inheritance route for later terminology. It does not establish that the framing was incorrect.
- It supports that the Healy opinion should be given strong weight on the questions actually asked while remaining neutral on issues not shown within the referral scope.
- It supports that the March 2023 receiving Neurology team was presented with genuinely conflicting causal information before later treatment decisions.
- It supports, on the surviving sources, that no single external referral text expressly assigns the complete mixed structural/dystonic/neuropathic/OFF-modulation proposition to one receiving service. This does not establish that no oral discussion or undocumented referral occurred.

---

**Pass 8 — supported but not established:**

- Repeated Parkinson-linked or neuropathic terminology may reflect a durable working model, but without a new examination, test, treatment-state observation or interpretation grounded in new evidence, repetition alone does not establish independent corroboration.
- The 2018 treating-neurologist account supports the specificity of the earlier procyclidine withdrawal history, but the record does not show whether that specificity came from a contemporaneous 2017 clinical observation, a later patient report, or another source not preserved here.
- The longitudinal record strongly supports true accumulation of medication-state evidence at the feet because the evidence comes from multiple distinct later events; it does not establish a single mechanism merely because the direction of some responses converges.

---

**Pass 9 additions — patient–clinician perspective reading:**

- The record supports that patient observations were repeatedly clinically informative, particularly when they described timing, withdrawal, OFF-state or symptom-domain dissociation. It does not establish that every patient causal interpretation was correct.
- It supports that clinicians sometimes preserved patient reports that did not match the observed clinic state, rather than simply rejecting them. It does not establish how representative any single clinic window was of the full day.
- It supports partial convergence by later 2023–2025 on medication-state modulation of pain/feet. It does not establish convergence on a single pain generator.
- It supports that the 2025 complaint represented a genuine difference in historical explanatory framing. It does not establish that either participant's retrospective account, taken alone, is a complete reconstruction of earlier knowledge.

---



**Pass 10 additions — supported but not established:**

The longitudinal record strongly supports that the foot symptom complex was neurologically modifiable and that this became increasingly important to management. It also supports that clinicians sometimes reached sufficient operational certainty to act — for example by reintroducing procyclidine, starting Stalevo for an OFF-related working formulation, changing evening levodopa timing, and trialling bedtime Sinemet CR — while leaving the underlying pain generator partly unresolved.

The record supports, but does not establish as a matter of clinical standard, that the accumulating medication-state evidence created repeated opportunities for explicit causal re-synthesis. Whether the remaining uncertainty was material enough at each stage to require different investigation or treatment is reserved for expert judgment.


**Pass 12 additions — supported but not established:**

The complaint response has substantial support for a long-running risk rationale and for Parkinson-related modulation of pain. It has more limited temporal support for the proposition that Sinemet's pain benefit was clearly understood from the beginning of levodopa use, and the surviving initiation record does not support pregabalin's original introduction as levodopa-sparing. The response's account of alcohol as one possible contributor is compatible with the presence of multiple alternative hypotheses, but the record alone does not establish the practical weighting of those hypotheses or resolve the complaint's anchoring allegation.

**Pass 11 additions — supported but not established:**

The record strongly supports medication-state modulation of at least part of the foot syndrome and a patient-specific narrow dopaminergic therapeutic window. It also supports a pregabalin-responsive pain component that was at least partly dissociable from toe curling/clawing. What those treatment responses **do not establish** is a unique pain mechanism, equivalence between procyclidine and levodopa effects, or that more dopaminergic stimulation would have been better at earlier dates.


**Pass 13 additions — supported continuity propositions:**

- The record supports a pattern in which some facts acquired **local management force before they acquired, or without visibly acquiring, global causal force**. The clearest example is the 2017–2018 procyclidine-related foot evidence.
- It supports that the treating record knew by 2018 that a focal outside-left-foot phenomenon had changed rapidly during neurological medication withdrawal. It does not establish what mechanism the treating team should have inferred from that fact.
- It supports that later 2023 OFF-related reasoning was based on genuinely new medication-state information rather than merely inherited terminology. The working model therefore changed prospectively at that point.
- It supports that the 2022 morning “warm-up” and 2025 morning medication-onset/CR pattern are temporally compatible. It does not establish that they represented the same mechanism or that the 2025 strategy was indicated in 2022.
- It supports that pregabalin-responsive pain and persistent toe curling were at least partly dissociable, which weakens any model that treats every foot manifestation as one identical symptom domain.
- It supports that the 2025 complaint response retrospectively presents a more continuous pain-modulation strategy than is explicitly visible in the intervening record. That does not make the later explanation inaccurate; it limits its temporal reach.

## 18. What the Record Does Not Resolve

**Pass 2 provisional unresolved questions:**

- The precise causal relationship among structural foot pathology, abnormal loading/posture, dystonia/Parkinsonism, peripheral neuropathic possibilities, pain modulation and pain amplification.
- The exact meaning that should have been assigned in 2017–2018 to the rapid return of foot symptoms during procyclidine withdrawal.
- When the treating team first formed a sufficiently clear view that levodopa was materially modifying foot pain, as distinct from motor function.
- Whether a levodopa timing/formulation/overnight-coverage trial should reasonably have been undertaken before 2025.
- Whether earlier genetic confirmation of PRKN/Parkin disease would have changed symptomatic management; the record alone cannot answer that counterfactual.
- Why the March 2023 self-withdrawal report appeared neutral while later OFF/ON descriptions and Sinemet CR response were much clearer.
- The precise mechanism of the Sinemet CR-associated foot-pain improvement.
- Whether the focal outside-left-foot pain described retrospectively was continuously present from the 2017 withdrawal episode through 2025.

---


**Pass 4 additional unresolved documentary questions:**

- The exact date on which the later handwriting on the April 2017 Podiatry referral form was added is not recoverable from the compiled record.
- The exact date of the reproduced 2017 clinician-to-Healy outgoing page is not visible in the source currently held.
- The original dispatch/wording of the historical 2017–2019 Podiatry appointment letters is not established by the 2026-generated copies alone.
- For undated year-split Neurology extracts, the exact clinic day remains unresolved unless another source supplies it.

**Pass 5 additional unresolved management questions:**

- What explicit threshold, if any, was intended to end the original levodopa-deferral policy?
- Whether the 2017–2018 medication-state evidence involving the feet should have triggered a documented re-synthesis of the governing treatment plan.
- Whether adverse effects on a dopamine agonist should have been treated as equally constraining for a carefully dosed levodopa strategy.
- Whether the timing/formulation/coverage options that became explicit in 2024–2025 should reasonably have been tested earlier.
- When pain first became a direct determinant of dopaminergic regimen design rather than a parallel symptom-management problem.
- Whether the distinct management transitions occurred at clinically appropriate evidential thresholds.

**Pass 6 additional unresolved referral questions:**

- The full content of the April/July 2017 communications with Podiatry, including any oral causal question not preserved in the record.
- Whether Podiatry received Neurology's explicit uncertainty that the corns/calluses/burning might be separate or neuropathic, rather than only the Parkinson's diagnosis/context.
- Whether Prof Healy discussed the foot-causation issue outside the surviving transcribed portions of his referral/response.
- The evidential basis for the 2019 Podiatry “No neuropathy” risk-status tick and whether it had any intended meaning beyond podiatric risk classification.
- Whether page 2 of the March 2023 urgent referral contained additional questions or clinical detail not present in the surviving transcription.
- Whether a chronic-pain, neuropathy or other specialist referral was subsequently made after it was discussed in 2025.


**Pass 7 additional unresolved causal questions:**

- The relative contribution over time of fixed/local structural pathology, dystonic posture, altered mechanical loading, peripheral-neuropathic processes and central/dopaminergic pain modulation.
- Whether a peripheral neuropathy was ever objectively established, and what evidential basis underlay later neuropathic language.
- The mechanism by which procyclidine withdrawal rapidly changed the outside-left-foot symptom complex.
- Why the March 2023 self-withdrawal report appeared neutral while later medication-timing/OFF evidence was positive.
- The mechanism of persistent toe curling despite substantial pregabalin-related pain relief.
- What the Sinemet CR response proves about symptom modulation versus pain generation.
- Which specific foot manifestations, if any, can reasonably be attributed to PRKN-related dystonia rather than to coexisting structural or other processes.

---

**Pass 8 additional unresolved evidential-development questions:**

- Which later uses of “neuropathic pain” were grounded in new objective neurological evidence and which were inherited working terminology?
- Whether the 2017 withdrawal evidence caused a contemporaneous re-appraisal of the July “Not clear” foot-causation proposition, even if that reasoning is not visible in the surviving record.
- The source of the added specificity in the April 2018 account of the outside-left-foot/corn return.
- Whether any 2021–2022 note not presently located documented levodopa-related foot-pain benefit before the explicit 2023 evidence.
- Which intervening Parkinson-linked foot descriptions represent genuine new observation, new interpretation, inherited framing, or retrospective reconstruction.

---

**Pass 9 additional unresolved perspective questions:**

- When the patient's own causal model of the foot pain changed, and whether he ever adopted a settled single-cause explanation rather than recognizing medication-state modulation.
- How consistently out-of-clinic patient reports of worse states were integrated into the longitudinal clinical model when clinic examinations occurred during better states.
- What shared explanatory model, if any, existed after the March 2023 disagreement and before the 2025 complaint.
- Whether discussions not visible in the surviving record narrowed the apparent difference between patient and clinician understandings of neuropathy, alcohol, Parkinson-related modulation and structural foot pathology.

---



**Pass 10 additions — unresolved closure questions:**

The record does not resolve when, if ever, the July 2017 foot-causation uncertainty was expressly closed before the complaint process. It does not establish that “neuropathic pain” meant proven peripheral neuropathy, nor that the 2019 Podiatry “No neuropathy” tick was a neurological causal adjudication. It does not determine what proportion of pain arose from structural lesions, dystonia/posture, altered loading, peripheral neuropathy, central pain modulation or interactions among them.

The record also cannot answer the counterfactual question of whether earlier timing/formulation or overnight-coverage changes would have produced the later Sinemet CR benefit without unacceptable dyskinesia. The later response changes the evidential state but cannot substitute for the prospective evidence available at earlier dates.


**Pass 12 additions — unresolved complaint-response questions:**

The record does not fix the earliest date at which Sinemet was explicitly understood as a foot-pain modifier, when pregabalin acquired a levodopa-sparing management role, how much practical diagnostic weight alcohol/peripheral-neuropathy framing received, or the exact start provenance of the March 2025 CR regimen. It also does not contain an independent clinical adjudication of whether earlier management caused preventable pain or whether the later CR strategy should reasonably have been tried sooner.

**Pass 11 additions — unresolved treatment-response questions:**

The record does not resolve why pregabalin efficacy fluctuated so markedly, whether the 2017 procyclidine foot response and later levodopa/CR pain response represent the same mechanism, what effect the 2024 pre-food/fluid timing change actually had, or how much of the 2025 CR benefit arose through dystonia, restored movement/loading, central pain modulation or another interacting process. It also cannot use later CR success to answer the counterfactual question of when the same formulation strategy should have been tried earlier.


**Pass 13 additions — unresolved evidential-persistence questions:**

- The record does not reveal whether the 2017–2018 foot treatment-state findings were mentally remembered but simply not repeated, or whether they ceased to influence later reasoning. Documentary silence cannot answer that.
- It does not determine what weight the 2018 focal outside-left-foot withdrawal finding deserved relative to persistent structural pathology.
- It does not establish whether the 2022 morning warm-up represented medication onset, mechanical warming, activity, footwear, natural fluctuation or a mixed mechanism.
- It does not resolve how the March 2023 ten-day self-withdrawal should be weighted against later missed-dose and Stalevo ON/OFF observations.
- It does not establish the outcome of the 2024 evening Sinemet timing/food/fluid experiment from the cited note.
- It does not determine whether the 2024 pain-versus-toe-curling dissociation should have changed the later referral, investigation or treatment architecture.
- It does not answer when accumulated evidence of dopaminergic pain modulation crossed a clinically meaningful threshold for testing timing/formulation/overnight coverage.
- It does not show whether earlier PRKN confirmation would have changed symptomatic treatment or the interpretation of the foot manifestations.

## 19. What Later Documents Reconstruct

**Pass 3 — retrospective audit:**

- The 25 March 2025 complaint response reconstructs a long-running understanding that Sinemet helps pain. The record clearly supports that understanding by 2023, but the located 2021 first-Sinemet note frames initiation around undertreatment and replacement of Kemadrin for ocular reasons rather than chronic foot pain.
- The same response reconstructs pregabalin as having been introduced partly to spare further Sinemet increase. The original 2023 record instead identifies GP initiation for severe burning/neuropathic-type pain. A levodopa-sparing function is clearly visible later, especially in 2025, so the later statement may accurately describe what pregabalin came to do without proving why it was first prescribed.
- The response states that dopaminergic responsiveness was “known.” The 2017 record more cautiously states that good levodopa response was possible and later specialist advice supported likely dopamine-replacement responsiveness in suspected PARK2. Subsequent treatment response makes the prediction look accurate retrospectively, but does not alter its original degree of certainty.
- The response's explanation that levodopa escalation was constrained by dyskinesia and other dopaminergic risks is supported by earlier contemporaneous documentation and by later observed adverse effects. Documentary support for the rationale does not itself answer whether each treatment decision was clinically optimal.
- The broad statement that management had been about Parkinson's from the outset is compatible with the overall neurological management history, but cannot be used to overwrite the separate 2017 statement that the relationship between the neurological disorder and the corns/calluses/burning foot symptoms was unclear.
- The 2026 genetic letter states that biallelic Parkin/PRKN disease was confirmed “as predicted.” That retrospective description is strongly supported by the 2017 PARK2 suspicion, while the confirmation itself belongs only to 2026.
- The present patient recollection that focal outside-left-foot pain was the first pain to return during procyclidine withdrawal has partial documentary corroboration in the 2018 clinician note; the direct 2017 email supports broader foot deterioration but not every detail of the later recollection.

These reconstructions remain evidence in their own right. Pass 3 changes their **evidential function** where their historical reach exceeds the contemporaneous material.

---

**Pass 8 evidential-development qualification:** The 2025 complaint response should not be treated as a new historical observation of 2017–2023. It is a new 2025 document that reorganises and explains earlier events. Its individual historical claims gain weight only to the extent that earlier contemporaneous records independently support them. The 2026 statement that PRKN disease was confirmed “as predicted” likewise contains two evidential layers: the new genetic result and a retrospective link back to the earlier hypothesis.

---

**Pass 9 perspective qualification:** The 2025 complaint and consultant response are best read as two participant accounts produced after years of treatment. The patient's complaint establishes his later experience and interpretation; the consultant response establishes the clinician's later explanation. Where they disagree about what was emphasized, understood or intended earlier, the earlier contemporaneous record remains the controlling evidence for temporal reach.


**Pass 12 complaint-response conclusion:** The response survives audit unevenly rather than failing or succeeding as a whole. The dyskinesia/young-age caution rationale is strongly corroborated. The pain-amplification model has genuine earlier antecedents. The Sinemet-pain claim is clearly supportable by 2023 but not shown as the 2021 initiation rationale. Pregabalin's later levodopa-sparing function is visible, but its GP-originated initiation for neuropathic-type pain remains distinct. Broad statements such as “known” responsiveness, “always” being open to error, and a single continuous strategy have a wider temporal reach than the surviving contemporaneous documentation directly establishes. The response's own language leaves the underlying pain cause open.

---



**Pass 13 continuity qualification on later reconstruction:**

The 2025 complaint response reconstructs a durable longitudinal understanding that Sinemet helped pain and that dopaminergic treatment had been cautiously managed because of adverse-effect risk. Pass 13 shows that these two historical strands have different carry-forward profiles. The adverse-effect strand is continuously visible from Neupro through low-dose levodopa, Stalevo and the later CR discussion. The foot-pain modulation strand is explicit in 2017–2018, becomes less visibly active when the pain recurs, and is then re-established through new 2023 and 2025 observations.

Accordingly, the complaint response may accurately capture the **eventual** clinical model while compressing how evidential continuity developed across time. Its retrospective continuity language cannot substitute for the intervening notes when deciding what was visibly active at each earlier decision point.

## 20. What Requires Expert Judgment

Pass 3 does not resolve the following external questions:

- whether the duration and sequencing of levodopa deferral remained clinically reasonable as treatment-response evidence accumulated;
- what causal meaning should be assigned to the 2017 procyclidine withdrawal/reintroduction foot effects;
- when an earlier trial of altered levodopa timing, formulation or overnight coverage would have been clinically indicated, if at all;
- what the later Sinemet CR response can establish about pain mechanism versus pain modulation;
- how structural foot pathology, dystonia/posture, abnormal loading, neuropathic processes and central/non-motor pain modulation should be apportioned;
- whether earlier PRKN confirmation would reasonably have altered treatment strategy;
- whether, and at what point, the referral architecture should have shifted from parallel service-specific management to a more explicitly targeted or multidisciplinary assessment of the mixed foot-pain mechanism.

These remain expert questions rather than documentary conclusions.

Pass 7 additionally reserves for external expertise:

- causal apportionment of structural lesion pain, dystonic/postural phenomena, abnormal loading, peripheral neuropathy and central/dopaminergic pain modulation;
- the evidential requirements for diagnosing peripheral neuropathy in the presence of a neuropathic pain phenotype;
- the clinical meaning of symptom dissociation between pregabalin-responsive pain and persistent toe curling;
- the mechanisms capable of producing the documented procyclidine and levodopa/CR treatment-state effects at the feet; and
- which foot manifestations are plausibly attributable to genetically confirmed PRKN-related dystonia.

---

Pass 8 additionally leaves for expert judgment whether newly emerging evidence at specific points—particularly the 2017 medication-withdrawal effect and the later 2023 OFF-related pattern—was sufficient to require a different clinical reassessment, investigation or treatment strategy. The register can identify when the evidential state changed; it cannot determine by itself what a reasonably competent specialist should have done with that change.

---


Pass 9 additionally reserves for external expertise:

- how much weight should ordinarily be given to repeated patient-reported OFF/time-of-day effects when the clinic examination samples a relatively good state;
- what valid inference could be drawn from the March 2023 ten-day self-withdrawal report and what structured assessment would have been needed to test it; and
- what level of shared explanatory formulation and documentation would ordinarily be expected where patient and clinician causal models diverge but treatment continues.

---



**Pass 11 treatment-response expert boundary:** External expertise is required to determine the mechanistic weight of the procyclidine withdrawal, the significance of non-monotonic dopaminergic responses, the meaning of pregabalin-related pain/motor dissociation, and the permissible causal or counterfactual inference from Sinemet CR success. Documentary analysis can identify the natural experiments and their confounders; it cannot convert response into mechanism or standard-of-care judgment.

**Pass 12 complaint-response expert boundary:** Documentary analysis can test the historical reach and internal support of the complaint response, but it cannot decide whether the supported risk rationale was implemented to an acceptable clinical standard, whether diagnostic weighting amounted to inappropriate anchoring, whether earlier formulation/coverage trials were indicated, or whether different management would probably have prevented the pain. Those questions require independent clinical expertise applied prospectively to each decision point.


**Pass 13 evidential-persistence expert boundary:** Documentary analysis can identify when a fact became newly knowable, whether it changed immediate management, and whether the same fact is later visible in the record. It cannot determine whether ordinary clinical practice required explicit re-citation, re-synthesis, re-testing, a different referral question, or an earlier timing/formulation trial. In particular, independent expert judgment is required on:

- the clinical weight of the 2017–2018 procyclidine-related foot findings;
- whether the 2022 morning temporal pattern should have triggered structured medication-state testing;
- how the March 2023 uncontrolled withdrawal should be reconciled with later positive OFF/ON observations;
- what the 2024 pain/toe-curling dissociation implies mechanistically and operationally;
- when timing, formulation and duration-of-coverage became a distinct clinically indicated variable from total levodopa dose; and
- whether the differing continuity of adverse-effect evidence and foot-modulation evidence has any significance beyond ordinary documentation variability.

## 23. Source-Fidelity Notes

Record transcription issues, addenda, handwritten annotations, unclear dates, duplicate entries, document layering, or any source problem that may alter interpretation.

- The 2017 GP export contains numerous duplicated clinical and administrative entries; duplicates are source-export repetitions and should not be counted as independent corroboration. (`complete-record.md`, CLIN-000809–CLIN-000814)
- Several year-split Neurology source pages state that exact clinic dates are absent in the extract and year assignment is based on internal chronology; exact dates should not be invented where the source does not print them.
- The Podiatry SAR transcription was revised against clearer native images. The 2017 referral form is layered, some handwriting is uncertain, and the referral date (10 Apr 2017), registration date (10 Jul 2017), and first-treatment date (14 Sep 2017) are separate events. (`complete-record.md`, CLIN-002821–CLIN-002839; CLIN-002997–CLIN-003017; CLIN-003217–CLIN-003219)
- The compiled record contains mojibake/OCR artefacts in some reproduced source text. Where a native-image correction or `[OCR unclear]` marker is supplied, that source-fidelity qualification remains controlling.
- Document 06 (Healy response) is a partial transcription of clearly legible passages from a photographed original; the original image remains controlling for wording not reproduced.
- Pass 1 does not treat later complaint correspondence as an authoritative account of earlier rationale; it is recorded only at its chronological date pending later audit.
- Pass 3 treats the 25 March 2025 complaint response as a retrospective explanatory document and tests each historical claim against earlier contemporaneous entries rather than accepting or rejecting the response globally.
- The present patient recollection in Document 07 is separately labelled from the contemporaneous 2017 emails; where a 2018 clinician note corroborates part of that recollection, the corroboration is recorded without converting the later recollection into a verbatim 2017 fact.
- The 2026 PRKN result is used retrospectively to assess the accuracy of the earlier PARK2 hypothesis, but it is not treated as knowledge available before 2026.
- Pass 4 distinguishes structured diagnosis fields from the narrative qualifications surrounding them; a categorical field is not treated as documentary proof that adjacent uncertainties were resolved.
- The 13 July 2017 DaTscan is expressly identified as an addendum; its position in the final document is not used to back-project the result into earlier reasoning.
- The 2019 Neurology material contains substantial reproduced 2017 text; carried-forward historical wording is not counted as a fresh 2019 observation unless the later note adds new evidence.
- The 2017 Podiatry referral page is treated as layered: the 10 Apr 2017 received stamp does not date every later visible annotation.
- Podiatry referral date, registration date and first-treatment date are preserved as separate administrative events.
- Appointment-letter pages generated in 2026 but displaying 2017–2019 appointment dates are treated as later representations of historical appointments, not automatically as contemporaneous surviving letters.
- The separate 2021 “Exact supplied text” excerpt is treated as another representation of the same clinical episode, not independent clinical corroboration.
- Where year-split pages lack exact source dates, or the clinician-to-Healy reproduced page lacks an exact visible date, the register preserves the available precision rather than inferring a day from compilation order.
- Pass 5 distinguishes a durable management constraint from a fixed management policy: the repeated concern about dyskinesia/ICD risk persists, while the operative use of levodopa changes from deferral to minimization, timing/demand adjustment and controlled-release coverage.
- Pass 5 does not infer missing decision criteria from later outcomes. Where the 2017 plan says levodopa may be needed “sooner or later” but supplies no fixed threshold, that threshold remains undocumented rather than reconstructed from hindsight.
- Pass 6 treats the 10 April 2017 Podiatry form as layered; later visible “PARKINSONS” or gait-related handwriting is not automatically dated to the original received stamp.
- The 6 July 2017 GP note records the outcome of a telephone discussion with Podiatry but does not preserve the full conversation; the register therefore does not infer unrecorded questions from the shorthand “as Parkinson's.”
- The clinician-to-Healy outgoing page has no exact visible date and the Healy response is a partial transcription; Pass 6 confines conclusions to the visible/transcribed referral scope.
- The March 2023 urgent Healthlink referral transcription is explicitly page 1 of 2. Pass 6 therefore does not assume that no additional question appeared on the untranscribed second page.
- Where no completed chronic-pain or neuropathy referral is visible, Pass 6 records documentary absence only and does not infer that no such referral occurred outside the surviving compilation.
- Pass 7 treats descriptive terms such as “burning,” “neuropathic,” “clawing,” “dystonia,” “OFF-related,” and “pain” as proposition-specific evidence, not as interchangeable diagnostic conclusions.
- Treatment response is used in Pass 7 to establish symptom modulation or dissociation only to the degree directly supported; it is not used as proof of a unique mechanism.
- The 2019 Podiatry “No neuropathy” tick is preserved as a service-specific risk-status entry and is not expanded into a definitive neurological exclusion without supporting assessment evidence.
- The 2026 PRKN letter is read according to its stated causal object—Parkinsonism and dystonia. Pass 7 does not enlarge that statement to every foot lesion or pain process.

- Pass 8 distinguishes serial re-examination from copied repetition: repeated Podiatry findings are treated as new observations because they arise from separate dated assessments, while copied-forward Neurology history is treated as inherited framing unless a new finding is separately identified.
- The April 2018 Neurology description of the 2017 procyclidine withdrawal is not counted as a second independent withdrawal experiment. It is later clinical documentation of the same underlying event, with additional specificity whose exact source is not visible.
- The 2019 Podiatry “No neuropathy” tick and Parkinson-linked wording are not treated as new neurological causal proof absent a clearly documented new neurological assessment supporting those statements.
- The March 2023 GP recollection of an earlier episode improving with better Parkinson control is classified as retrospective evidence about that earlier episode; the severe current pain and ten-day medication-interruption report are classified separately as new 2023 evidence.
- The 25 March 2025 complaint response is new evidence of the clinician's 2025 explanatory position only. Its claims about earlier knowledge, intent or strategy remain backward-reaching propositions requiring contemporaneous support.
- The February 2026 PRKN result is treated as genuinely new confirmatory evidence and is not merged with the earlier PARK2 hypothesis into one continuous state of certainty.

- Pass 9 treats patient causal language such as “habitual,” “neuralgic,” or “not Parkinson related” as evidence of the patient's belief at that time, not as objective diagnostic proof.
- Pass 9 treats clinician descriptions such as “OFF-related,” “neuropathic,” or pain “magnification” as clinician interpretations unless independently established by examination/test evidence appropriate to that proposition.
- A clinician-authored note that records a patient report remains evidence that the report was documented; it does not erase the distinction between patient-origin phenomenology and clinician-origin interpretation.
- Where the patient and clinician appear to agree on treatment effect, Pass 9 does not infer agreement on mechanism unless the source expressly records it.
- Apparent disagreement between a patient's reported state and a clinic examination is preserved as a possible observation-window issue rather than resolved by privileging either account.
- The 2025 complaint correspondence is treated as participant perspective evidence at its own date. The patient's complaint and clinician response are not used as substitutes for contemporaneous records of earlier discussions.

---


- Pass 10 treats absence of an explicit causal-resolution statement as “no closure located in the surviving record,” not proof that no undocumented reasoning or discussion occurred.
- The 2019 Podiatry “No neuropathy” tick is preserved within its source field and is not upgraded into a neurological exclusion unless the source itself establishes that scope.
- The 2025 complaint response uses conditional language about peripheral neuropathy and “whatever the underlying cause”; Pass 10 therefore does not treat that document as final causal closure of the foot-pain generator.
- The 2026 PRKN letter explicitly closes genetic causation of Parkinsonism and dystonia; its wording is not extended beyond those stated propositions to every foot lesion or pain mechanism.


- Pass 11 treats treatment changes as potential natural experiments only to the degree the changed variable and subsequent response are documented. A medication change without a recorded outcome remains an attempted experiment, not evidence of benefit.
- The 2017 procyclidine withdrawal is stronger response evidence than later missed-dose anecdotes because the interruption was planned and contemporaneously described; the 2018 outside-left-foot specificity is later documentation of the same episode and is not double-counted as a second experiment.
- The March 2023 pregabalin accounts are preserved as separate timepoints even though their apparent efficacy differs. Pass 11 does not force them into a single stable response narrative.
- The 2023 darts video is explicitly treated as a confounded observation window because extra levodopa and alcohol were on board and the patient said it was not representative of usual evenings.
- The 2024 evening Sinemet timing change is not assigned an outcome that the cited note does not provide.
- The 2025 CR response is used to support formulation/coverage sensitivity and pain modulation only; it is not used as proof of a unique pain generator or of an earlier counterfactual outcome.


- Pass 12 treats the 25 March 2025 complaint response as a substantive primary source for what the consultant said and believed in 2025, but not automatically as primary contemporaneous evidence of what was known or intended in 2017–2023.
- Direct quotations from 2017 embedded in the complaint response are checked against the earlier source and retain the earlier wording's evidential status; the 2025 connective interpretation around those quotations remains retrospective.
- The 15 April 2025 Quality and Patient Safety letter is used as evidence of complaint framing and administrative routing only; its summary of the patient's allegation is not converted into an independent clinical finding.
- “Peripheral neuropathic pain symptomatologies” is preserved as the complaint response's wording and is not silently converted into an objectively established peripheral-neuropathy diagnosis.
- The response's current report of profound Sinemet CR relief is separated from its uncertainty about the exact initiation route so that outcome evidence is not discarded because provenance details remain incomplete.
- Broad attitude/continuity terms such as “always,” “known,” and “for quite some time” are treated as propositions requiring temporal support rather than as self-proving historical facts.

- Pass 13 uses **visible carry-forward** as a documentary concept only. A later note's failure to repeat an earlier fact does not establish that the clinician did not remember or consider it.
- The 2017 procyclidine email and 2018 Neurology note are treated as different evidential layers of the **same withdrawal episode**. The 2018 focal specificity is not double-counted as a second independent experiment.
- The March 2023 ten-day medication interruption is preserved exactly as reported: “no difference from a Parkinson's perspective.” It is not silently rewritten as “foot pain did not change.”
- The 2022 morning warm-up is treated as a temporal observation, not an undocumented medication-onset test.
- The 2024 pregabalin pain/toe-curling dissociation is preserved as a symptom-domain observation; it does not prove that either symptom had a unique mechanism.
- The 2025 CR result is treated as evidence about **timing/formulation/duration of dopaminergic coverage**. It is not generalized backward into proof that earlier immediate-release treatment was an equivalent test.
- Structural Podiatry findings and neurological medication-state responses are kept in parallel. Evidence of one is not used to erase the other.
- The PRKN/PARK2 trajectory is used as a positive-control example of proposition-level continuity: suspicion and confirmation remain temporally separate.
- “Loss of evidential continuity” in Pass 13 means only that the earlier fact is no longer visibly active in the surviving later working model. It is not a finding of clinical fault.

---

# Governing Rule

> The purpose of this register is not to produce a cleaner story than the source record supports. It is to preserve the distinction between what happened, what was documented, what the record itself reveals about that documentation, what can reasonably be inferred, and what remains for expert judgment.

A finding should be capable of being challenged at each level:

**source → case fact → record fact → interpretive consequence → unresolved proposition → expert question.**

If any link cannot be shown, the conclusion should be weakened accordingly.
